                           The sky was quite heavily overcast
             it was raining at the time . I pulled my vehicle
              to the side of the road in behind another vehicle
              and the gentleman asked if I had seen it and what I
              thought it was. I told him I didn't think it could
              be anything except a plane . 1 did not hear any noise
              f rom this object as I was not out of the car . This
              object was very bright; i~ was much brighter than the
              moon . lt was red and then it became a glaring light
              and then got bigger and ~as r ound- shaped. It then
              burst and then all we saw was smoke . From the first
              time I saw it until it burst approximately two minutes
              passed by . This object appeared to be approx . 1000
              feet above the trees and was not travell ing in an arc
              but on a horizontal plane and it did not appear to
              travel very far before it exploded .
              Witness:   Cst . M.J. Dundon              Signed:

          • • • , , • WER.IIJ ORI VlNG TOWARDS Inverness from 'l'ruro on highway
                      ffl9 and when we were in the vicinity of Hugh MacLEID~ 1 s
                      garage I saw a ball of fire in the sky off to our
                      left. It was a couple of miles away and a couple of
                      miles high . This ball of fire was as bright as the
                      sun and ap~roximately the same size. We observed this
                      thing for a few minutes . The object never stopped but
                      dropped towards the earth in a straight line . While
                      the ball of fire was present there was smoke all
                      around it and all of a sudden the fire disappeared and
                      then I saw a black object ~hich turned grey and then
                      white and fell to the ground . It did not drop in a
                      straight line then but appeared to ~1aver sideways as
                      it fell . It might not have been two miles away. It
                      was between the road and the mountain.

                                      5 p. m. on the 12 MAY 71, I was with
      --                               both from Bishop's Falls, I was
      driving                  s       on the Trans Can!lda Highway .
      Approximately 3 miles East of Grand Falls .llll noticed so~thing
      and brought my attention to it . I noticed a bright,orange coloured
      object, oval in shape off to my right. The object appeared to be
      in the air, about 500 - 1000 ft. to my right . From sitting in the
      car it was eye level when I looked through the passenger window
      to my right. I couldn't say if it was in the air or on the ground.
      There was a hill in the background so it is hard to say if the object
      was in the air or on the ground. The object was about 8 ft . long
      and about 4 ft . wide. It was oval shape . It didn't appear to
      light up anything around it, giving out a constant light , It was
      a clear night, no fog, mist or clouds. I viewed the object
      for about 2 minutes , It appeared to be stationary and while we
      were stopped the object appeared to fold up and just disappeared.
      It didn't move but just disappeared in the same spot, The oueline
      of the object was smooth, not irregular.

            Fort McMurray, Alberta.                  22 APR 71



                                                                     22 APR 71

            1.           While on patrol of Fort McMurray Detachment a rea, an object was
            observed in the sky at a poi nt approximately 14 miles south of Fort l.foMurray.
            This object, a brightly shining sphere, travelled towards the earth, leaving
            a short white trail behind it. At a height approximately half a mile above
            the horizon it turned into a bluish flash, then disappeared. At the time of
            t his obse rvation the Northern Lighte were i n full display in the northeast
            sky, wl\ile the object wne observed in the southwest sky.

                                                                     23 APR 71
            2.                   The following telex dispatched to Edmonton Sub/Divisionl

                   Unidentified Flying Object Sighting
                   Wardlow District , Alberta ~ 17 FEB 71.




                  l.           Mr.                of Wardlow, Alberta contacted
                  this office to a            he and his wife sighted what they          ,.
                  thought to be an unidentified flying object . T e si
                                                                  ~     hting
                  occurred at approx. 5:25 A. M. 17 FEB 71. Mrs .           was
                  up feeding her daughter when the lights started to     c er
                  off and on . She then l ooked out the window and saw the
                  object . It is described as a large round object , dull orange
                  in colour with a line around same .approx. ~way down from ,
                  the top . The object appeared to be approx. 400 feet from
                  the ground . She got her husband out of bed and they both
                                                                                   ·-....
                                                                                   .·~·


                  observed same for approx. 15 minutes and then went to bed .      ,·''..
                                                                                    iH

                  When they got up at approx. 6:30 i.M., the object was gone.        '

                   2.           The sky was clear at the time and the sun was
                                                                                    ..·-
                                                                                    ....
                   rising. They were looking east towards the sun and the            ••
                                                                                     r,.
                                                                                     •
                   object appeared to be to the right and above the sun. The
                   sun had not appeared over the horizon. There was no move-
                   ment to the object and it could be seen clearer with the
                   naked eye than with a pair of binoculars. The location of
                   the sighting woUld be approx. 6 miles south of Wardlow,
                   Alta . along the Jted Deer river.
                   3.          Brooks Telex Number 34 was disp~ this
                   regard. This office has had dealings with lllllllllf in
                   the past and he is of reputable character. It might be
                   noted that the lights at ~ residence have been
                   blinking off and on prior~ce this incident and
                   same cannot be definitely related to the sighting. In the
                   opinion of the writer and Sgt. PINNOCK who assisted in this
                   investigation it is felt that the U.F.O . was only a sun
                   dog or something of sim.iliar nature , No one else could be
                   located that saw the sighting.

         On Saturday morning 1 9 JAN 71, I left home at about twenty-five to
   eight and travelled to the Success Canpressor Station - Saskatchewan POorer
  Just past Oantuar I observed an object ahead of me to the left . It was
   quite a ways ahead of me. It was five hundred to a thousand teet high.
   It was travelling towards the town of Success . It had white l ight, dull
  like roo. get through a light shade. It was long and narrow . My impressiar
  was that it vas about eighteen inches wide and quite long. It appeared to
  be flat surfaced. It went all the way to Success in this form and I
   turned on the turn off to the Compressor Station. I could s ee the object
  go over Success and then it turned and headed towards Caswell ' s farm,
  ak!.cut two miles !).0orn the roe.d I was travelling on. It was now in a
  different form - it appeared to be a round disc layi ng horizontally with
  a second smaller disc under it . The discs were of a yellow light. This
  was easy to see, however, it was not a blinging light .
         As I approached Caswell' s farm, the object met me there . It changed
  to a deep wall. of mixed colours, the shape was irregular 1 with the lights
  moving vertically, tbe sight simitiar to that in northern lights. It
  was over Caswelll s yard and f aded out over the trees . When I got past
  Caswell's yard, I saw it again in the shape it came across the field fr~a
  Success . It was about a quarter of a mile ahead of me on the right, about
   one hundred feet off the ground, or lower, and it illuminated the ground
  a bit . I went to the turn off and it veered to the right, going around
  the edge of the Compressor Station Sight.         Near the north east corner of
  the Station Sight it changed to a red ball, similiar to a r ed sunset,
  with a line across the bottom . The colour appeared to move.
         I drove into the yard. I shut the engine off and left the lights on.
  This red object stopped in front          the car between the tanks and trees,
  I went into the building and told                               working .
  -          came out and looked at i t .       c                  the maintenance
  ~ I later told him to go to the windcw and                        me the object
  appeared to stay between the t anks and trees . It stayed there about
  fifteen minutes .      It disappeared into the distance - it just faded out .
  It seemed to r etain the same ball shape a nd colour . The size was bigger
  than the sun .
         When the object ~as ahead of me on I 32 highway I switched my lights
  off and on. Tbe object here must have been travelling at one hundred
  miles per hour or more.
        The sky was overcast with the air being clear a~d peaceful ,
         The object never left a trail behind . I did not bear any sounds as
  the compressor sight has an abundance of noise.
         On Saturday afternoon I checked the areas by the tanks and found
  nothing out of the ordinary.
  Witnessed a   (C.A. Lentowicz) Cst.        Signed a

          G.R. BAMBURI patrolled to the area. He could observe the object,
          but not in the det&il described by the observer in the early A.M.
          This could be atributed to its high altitude.

          Fireball Sighting - Little Hearts Ease,
          T.Bay, Newfoundland, - 14 AUG 70

          l.         Receipt is acknowledged of Form A-151-l from o.c. St.
          John's Sub/Division, dated 15 SEPT 70.
                                                      14 AUG 70
           2.         At 9:00 p.m. , a phone call wae received from Mr.llllllll
          -      of Little Hearts Ease, Nfld. He advised that he and ~
          ~ had just seen an object fall out of the sky and it looked to
          tham aa if it was a plane or something similar and it was on fire .
          3.          An        te patrol was made to the scene by the under-
          signed and the        family wae contacted. They advised me' that ··.
          their attention           been attracted to the object by the rUshing
          sort of noise it gave o££. When they saw it 1 it was travellingJin · ·
          a North Easterly direction. It paeaed directly over the settlemen:f; .
          of Little Hearts Ease at a height of approximately tt mile and. then ·.
          it abruptly crashed into the mouth of the harbour. · Just prior to . ..
          crashing, it commenced a wobbling sort of motion and pieces seemed
          to fly off it . They described it as being about eight to ten feet ...
          in length, With a taU extending several feet beyond this. n was
          described as being blood red in colour, with the tail being a lighter
          shade of red. As it passed over the set~lement, it gave out
          sufficient light to light up the settlement and distinct shadows
          could be seen.
          4.            The weather conditions and visibility at t he time were ·
          very good. The sky was starlit and dark had fallen. Shortly after
          my arrival at the scene, a patrol was made by boat to the crash site
          and the a r ea wa~etely searched. Nothing was found anywhere in
          the area. Mr.lllllllladvised me that he would be fishing in the
          area the next day and be would bring it to the a~tention of others
          fishing in the area and should they find anything, they would notify
          me immediately . This search was made by them, but nothing was found .
          5.          The information obtained on the immediate patrol was
          passed on to Sgt . A.O . MacPHEE via radio and he made a check with
          the Air Terminal in Gander . After a short time, they were able to
          advise us that no planea were overdue or missing anywhere in the
          Province of Newfoundland.

          a full confirmation o£ tne sighting . The description given and the
          time o! sighting matohed~ail . The persons making this
          ~~e local - -' and her husband, Mr. and Mrs .

                                NATIONAL RESEARCH COUNCIL OF CANADA
                              CONSEIL NATIONAL DE RECHERCHES DU CANADA

                                                                        OTTAWA 7.

                                                                        June 18, 1970.



                  Inspector R.B. Muir,
                  Criminal Investigation Branch,
                  The Commissioner,
                  Royal Canadian Mounted Police,
                  Ottawa 1, Ontario.
                  Dear Inspector Muir:
                           Thank you for the copy of the report fr0111- - dated
                  May 21, 1970. We have looked at the two colour pho~losed
                  with the report, and our conclusion is that the object referred to is
                  probably the reflection of a fl~escent light in plate glass. Since
                  the pictures were taken in February the temperature would be low, and
                  it is highly probable that t he photographer was either on the bridge
                  or an enclosed promenade deck or in the lounge. In any of these cases ,
                                           .   .      .
                  he would have been photographing through piate g1ass.
                            Whatever the explanation, it is practically certain that the
                  image on the photographs is the result of either a double exposure or
                  a 'reflection, Any object that prominent and of that apparent size
                  that was actually in the sky would have been seen by the photographer.
                  Tho report reads that 11he saw, nor heard nothing at the time.'" We
                  have placed this on our records, and will return the photographs as
                  soon as copies have been made. Thank you for your cooperation in
                  sending this to us •
                          •
                                                          Sincerely yours,




                                                          Peter M. Millman.

                  Uniden~ified                Flying Object- Johnville, Carl . Co., N.B.


            h.        Further to our radiogram #&:J                                                            , please be
            advised that at approx. 2:15 PM 10 May 70                                                           Holmesville,
            N.B. called and reported what be thought                                                        crash in the
            Johnville area. He noticed something falling in                                               sky which appeared
            to be on fire and trailed by heavy black smoke.
            2.        S'rATE.MENT OBTAINED FROlr1 - -(B.- -) - OF
            HOLMESVILLE, N.B. 12:30 PM ll l~y ~
                      At approx. 1:30 PM ll May 70, I was in my store at Holmesville,
            N.B. when I heard some children outside playing say " look at that". So
            with that I walked over to the window andlooked out. I looked out the
            window in a South-east direction and saw an object that looked like an
            aircraft. The reason I say it was a plane is because I could see the
            tail section but that is all I could make out because the rest of it was
            covered in flames and was trailed by he~vy black smoke. Tbe . tail section
            appeared to be grayish in color~ This object was decending to the earth
            very rapidly at approx, 45 degrees in a gliding fashion. All of a sudden
            the flames died out and this dense smoke appeared to dr itt ott in a
            southerly direction. This object then seemed to go into a spin and nosed
            itself to the ground where it disappeared out ot sight . From the time I
            first saw this object until it disappeared out of sight I did not hear
            any noise whatsoever or any type of explosion.· I would say that from the
            first time I saw this object until it appeared to have crashed to the
            ground) last~d for a duration of approx. five minutes. Just about the
            time this object disappeared we could smell smoke that had been emitted
            from this object . This smoke seemed to give off an odour like a gunpowder
            smell. The distance from where I was standing to where I feel the object
            landed would be approx, five or six miles in a Southeast direction, which
            should put the object in the vicinity of Shepherd's Hill.
            3.        A patrol and search of the woodsu' in Johnville, N. B. regarding
            a possible plane crash met with negative r~sults. Also a check was made
            in the area with owners of small private aircraft, airportg in the
            Province and a check was also made with the IJ!aine state police in trying


~~
            to confirm if any small aircraft had been reported missing but these
            efforts met with negative results as well.
            4.         In view of investigation and the lack of information as to what


 i
            this object might be,it was being treated as a U. F. O. However two days
            lat~r from the time this complaint was brought to the attention of this
            office I was conductin~   n uiries in bhe Holmesville area when I heard a
            rumor that the son of                 , whose home is located in Holmesville ,
 iE         N.B . had set offa rocket in   s back yard on Sunday 10 f~y 70 relative to
 g.         the time that the complainant called this office advising of a possible
            plane crash.

                              2 APR 70
  1.       At approximately 4:15 A . ~t . while on rout inn patrol on Highway
  #1 6, approximately 5 miles from the city , a n Unidentified Flying Ob)cct
  was sighted by Cpl . A. R. TOMLINS and myself .
  2.      The U.P. o. appeared to be comet like in description , a whitish
  yellow star like object (very bright) 1 with an arched rooster type tail,
  also whitish yellow in colour which was extremely long . No noticeable
  movement could be disce.r ned in the object. The altitude indicated that
  the objec t was most probably in outer spcl.Ce . 'l'he object was viewed by
  myself and Cpl . TOMLINS for at least t wenty (20) minutes and eventually
  faded with the coming dawu . It may also be noted that at times during
  the viewing the object a ppeared to grow dimmer then brighter .
  3.       Prince Rupert is in the Pacific Standard Time Zone and the
  condltion of the sky in tho area of the U.F . O, was clear . Although as
  stated , no movement of this object was noted , hoth observers are satis-
  fied that the sighting was that of an object in outer space and not that
  of a condition brought about by the r eflection of light from an unknown
  source on a 101~ lying c loud ,

             2.          The sky was clear and the sun was shining bright-
             ly. They were looking south into the sun and the object ap-
             peared to be to the left and below the sun. I t appeared to be
             circular with a flat top and bottom. The object was longer
             than it was deep and was silver in colour. There was no move-
             ment except for a few fluctuations in light colour, possibly
             due to the sun reflecting on same. When t he object disappeared
              it proceeded almost straight up at a fast rate of speed and
             nniahed be.fore leaving the field o.f the scope. The location
             of the sighting would be approx. 2) miles north of Brooks, Alta.
             and the object appeared to be just west or the Brooks Townsite.

                                                        Melita




                                       About a quarter after five, I was north ot Melita,
                       on ~A1n St . on the 16 Dec 69. I went on west of town about three
                       quarters of a mile, I got out of the Gar to have a good look at
                       it. I had seen a bright light South West of Melita about fifteen
                       to twenty miles away. It was heading 1n a westerly direction,
                       Right after I stopped a Pick-up stopped behind me and myself and
                       two men watched it for about two to tour minutes , It was no Comet
                       or plane and we could not figure out what it was ,
                                          It was quite bright, somewhat larger than a small
                       plane, Lights appeared to be on the s1d~ of it in a straight l ine.
                       You could not tell the exact shape because it was to far away, It
                       was a light bright orange 1n colour. The lights stayed oonstant arid
                       did not blink, It stayed on the course and did not change at anytime,
                       We.... could net hear any sound •
                                      The sky was clear, no clouds , red sunset, still with
                       no wind, about 20 degrees above zero at the time ,

!'""'

                       and watched 1t .

            Unidentified Flying Object
            Perth, Ontario .



                                15 DEC 69
 1.      Miss . . _, age -         Perth , Ontario, con t acted this
 office and re~ on l2DEC 69, at approximately 11: 55Ptl[ (ES'r)
 she saw an unidentified flying object .
 2.      - - , reported that the above occurred while she
 was walk~, about three quarters of a mile north of her
 residence and of the junction of Highways 7 and 15, she described
 the object as being silver in colour about 10 ft . in diameter
 with some sort of light attached to !t. Miss - - watched the
 object for a £e-.r seconds, then became frightened and returned to
 her home; she noted that the object floated or drifted slowly at
 tree top level. The sky at this time was dark and cloudy.
 3·      Interviewing Miss- - further, I questionned her as to
 whether or not the "object" that s he saw could have been some sort
 of weather balloon; she felt that the object could well have been
 a large balloon.

  4•      I contacted the Uplands Weather Office ~d found out that
  many weather balloons are released in this area. Accor4ing to the
        r Office the above description of the "Object" seen by Miss
         fitted that of a "Pilot Balloon", which is about 8ft in
•       er, equipped with a small light and various instruments .
  These instruments measure wind velocity and a transmitter relays
  the information back to t he base at which point ther are used to
  compile weather reports. These balloons are common y released
  from a base at Trenton, Ontario, on a regular basis. According
  to the forecaster winds and weather conditions at the time that
  the "Objeot" was sighted would account for its being in the Perth
 area .
 5·       The "Object" was obviously a weather balloon, since it was
 not reported until several days after it was sighted, a telex i·ras
 not f orwarded as per Operational l•1 anual.
 6,      Although there have been no other ~of this U .F , Q.
 there is no apparent reason to doubt Miss ,........ reliability .

ON 8DEC69 AT 4.05PM MST A                                    OF 1639 28TH
AVE SW CALGARY ,ALTA. AND HIS WIFE                          CAME INTO THIS
OFFICE AND REPORTED THAT      THEY HAD SEEN THE ABOVE APPROX 5 Ml E OF
KINDERSLEY,SASK ON HWY 7. AT THE TIME THE SKY WAS A CLOUDY GREY
HAZE. IT WAS FIRST OBSERVED AS A LARGE ORANGE GLOWING BALL ON THE
HORIZON . IT THEN FLATTENED OUT INTO A RECTANGULAR SHAPE STILL
ORANGE AND GLOWING AND THEN DISAPPEARED INTO THE SW QUITE RAPIDLY
WITH PULSATIONS OF PINK REMAINING. IT WAS APP~OX 1/2-tMIAWAY
FROM THEM AND APPEARED TO BE 50-100' ABOVE THE GROUND. IT WAS SEEN
FROM 2-4 MINUTES.
                     KINDERSLEY DET

                                             MOHCTOH
                     Unidentified Flying Objects
                     Westmorland County, N. B.
                     2/5 NOV 73

                     Further to our massage no. 73/491, 6 IIOV 73, this is to
         advise that logical explanations have been discovered for all reported
         U.P.O. sightings in Westmorland County, N. B.
         2.           It was learned from airport authorities this point, that
         aircraft destined for Moncton or departing from same have been instructed
         to leave all runninq lights and landing lights illuminated for a
         predeterained distance. Thus weighing the facta available of the
         aightings , as well aa the fact that aircraft destined for Halifax,
         N. s., would be using~ approach located over the vicinity of sightJngs
         noted on message no. 73/491 as A, c, D, and P, it is concluded that the
         object observed vas an aircraft. Optical illusion effects and
         at.ospheric conditions at the time of the sightings would account for
         the persumption that the objects (aircraft) were close to the observer
         and also the absence of sound.
         3.           Thus, reported sighting on the 4/5 •ov 73 have been concluded
         as having been observations of the even1nq star Venus, which was
         learned to be at ita brightest illuaination, in this reqion and time
         of year. It was also learned that the star could be observed for a
         period of approxiaately 2~ hours coaaencing at 6:30 p.m. at its moat
         brilliant. Authorities advised that the star would appear to the human
         eye to hae a flickering effect and gases released from s ame as well as
         certain atmospheric conditions present would account for the changes
         in col or . Therefore these facts would account for sightinga No. •a•
         and "B".
         4.          In view of the above, it is the opinion of the writer that
         all reported aightinga may be said to be as noted, and it is felt that
         no further action is necessary.



         CONCLUDED HERE.

                                          - UFO Report
                                  Sask.        27 OCT 72


      1 NOV 72

      1.        I attended at the . . residence at Estlin, Sask. ,
      regarding the reported sighting of a UFO and the following
      information wae learned:
                (a) The sighting was at 10:30 PM CST of 27 OCT 72
                (b) It was a very cloudy night with thick cloud
                      cover
                (c) The observers are identified as follows:



                                                Housewife
                            (d)   The sighting was approx. 10 miles south, Ji miles
                                  east and 2 miles north of Regina, Sislc.
                            (e)   There were no other persons witness to the
                                  sighting.
                            (f)   When first observed, the UFO was somewhat of a
                                  disk shape and a pale yellow color; however, it
                                  changed to a very brilliant orange. When it pro-
                                  gressed to the orange color, it began moving at
                                  a very acute angle in an easterly direction until
                                  it completely disappeared. An altitude could not
                                  be determined; however, the illumination produced
                                  was bright enough to outline trees in the area. A
                                  large orange mist was present for approx. 10 to 15
                                  minutes a.fter the UFO left the area.
                            (g)   The duration of the observation was approx. four
                                  minutes.
      2.        It is my personal opinion afte    nterviewing both
      subjects that they are well-educate~d espons1ble citizens ,
      and that the information is from a re    b e so~ce. Telexes
      dispatched as per instructions ~

                           U.F.O . Report
                           South~lest Ottawa




               On the 27 SEP 72, information was received at "A" Division
     Desk from Cst. Ralph THORHAUGE, member of our Force that his wife
     had sighted a U.F.O . in the western area of Ottawa .
 2.        I interviewed Mrs. THORHAUGE on 28 SEP 72 and the
 following details were obtained . She was in her residence,
 25 Saginaw Crescent, Ottawa and happened to look through the
 study room window facing the western direction. She sighted a
 very bright light i n an oval shape in the sky. She observed this
 object for five minutes and then called her neighbour, Mrs. Leigh
 ANDERSON. Mrs. ANDERSON was accompanied by her husband who also
 is a member of the Force; both ANDERSONS looked through their
 residence window and spotted this U.P.O. The same description
 of the U.F.O. was obtained from the ANDBRSONS . The observers
 were not able to determine an approxiwate altitude of the U.F.O.
 but at the time the sky was clear and the approximate time of
 sighting was between 6: 45 p.m. and 7:00 p.m. on 27 SEP 72, East-
 ern Daylight Time.
     3.        The U.F.O. was sighted in the western direction and
     £rom the observers' point of view, the U.F.O. slightly was
     descending toward the southerly direction. The u.r.o. disappeared
     from the sight of the observers at approximately 7:00 p.m.
     4.        All observers who reported sighting this U. F . O. are
     believed to bo reliable sources.



     CONCLUDED HERE:
     (~/SGT.
     NCIQ)I/C GIS
     /lja

                                         CALGARY                 1~1<'-L.J"DD- UFO
                                         BROOKS !.ruN. DET.    l-172 -400-19

                                - Sighting of U, F, O.         JIQ .. · zru:c-:t;;s-··
                                  10 AUG 72 .
                                                                       NO RECOR D


                                                                        CIB INDEX
            l.           At 2:45 P. M. ,               of Calgary, Alta,,
            came to the Brooks Det .         to report that he had sighted
            an u.r. o. Th~   articulars of the object were obtained from
            - · Mr.                      , Brooks, Alta ., who was with - ,
            a lso confirme    e s g t ng. Shortly thereafter , numerous
            reports were received from residents in Brooks and the sur-
            rounding area, The object was described as appearing to be on
            fire and had various bright colours in it . It was high in the
            sky and travelled parallel to the ground . A trail of smoke
            followed the object across the sky. Some observers described
            i t as being round in shape, moving from a south- west direction
            to a north-east direction.
            2.           At 3:00 P. M. this date a phone call was placed to
            the Armed Forces Base at Suffield , Alta. , enquiring about the
            sighting . They did not have any knowledge of what the object
            might be . The Traffic Control Tower at the Calgary International
            Airport was also contacted . They advised that they had received
            var ious reports of sightings in the Calgary area . They could not
            provide information as to what the object might have been , Brooks
            message #234 was dispatched .
            3.           Later in the day of 10 AUG 72 , the sighting was the
            subject of news broadcasts . Scientists in the United States
            believe that a meteo~ite had entered the earth ' s atmospher on
            a f lat path and had caused the unusual sighting. The meteorite
            is believed to be the remains of a comet . It i s the opinion of
            the writer that the sighting at and near Brooks, Alta., is the
            same object as the meteorite , None of the persons reporting
            the sighting deny this possibility. There is no further action
            anticipated at this point .

                     Near Morrin, Alberea. 10 AUG 72.


                                            10 AUG 72
   1.        At approximately 2:30 P.M. on Thursday, I was on routine
   patrol in the Morrin district, Alberta. At the junction of No. 27
   and No. 6              I     occasion to check two hitch hikers ,
                               Bz         and his brother, -
                                    ,      of Forestburg, Alberta,
               cni!CK 1 one of the subjects called attention to the
                    0 was approaching our position from the south ,
   travelling in a northerly direction. Its altitude was estimated
   at between 4 to 5 thousand feet and travelling at a very high
   rate of speed. When first observed, the U F 0 appeared to be
   climbing and then to level off, spiralling as it travelled. It
   appeared to be silver in color with flashes of blue and green
   noted. A smoke trail was noticed behind the U.F.O. when firs~
   observed and continued for the full duration of observation
   estimated at 15 to 20 seconds. It then appeared to disintegrate
   in level flight as no explosion was noted. Weather conditions
   at the time were clear and hot.
    2.        Drumheller Detachment Telex Message No. 139 was dis-
    patched outlining the occurrence as per Op , Man, CO-air.
    3.        As no further action is aemed necessary, this file will
    be shown as hereunder noted .
    CONCLUDED HERE:
